## 11.15 Production process supervision and exit evidence, July 22, 2026

The affected assets are service availability, active user work, and bounded operational metadata across the Railway container, Tini, process supervisor, application child, PostgreSQL, and provider restart boundaries. The production image previously built `dist/process-wrapper.mjs` but launched `dist/index.mjs` directly, bypassing the only component designed to observe child PID, boot identity, exit code, signal, and restart decisions. Independent from the original death cause, that gap made an application-child failure indistinguishable from whole-container disappearance and prevented the existing watchdog contracts from owning recovery.

**Closed in source.** Tini remains PID 1. The shell and package production entrypoints now execute the built supervisor, which launches only `dist/index.mjs`, forwards SIGTERM/SIGINT, preserves child stdout/stderr and IPC, applies the existing health/watchdog predicates, and bounds same-container restart attempts with exponential backoff before exiting non-zero to Railway. One structured `process_lifecycle` envelope records wrapper identity, child PID/path/boot, observed code/signal, clean-versus-unclean termination, and restart decision/count. `runtime-process-lifecycle.ts` stores one fenced current/previous boot record per provider-issued environment/service/replica runtime key in the existing global `system_settings` operational store. `server/index.ts` is the sole application signal coordinator: it stops admission, timers, and executor supervision; aborts active Agent work through its existing owner; drains HTTP/browser work within a fixed budget; records clean termination; closes PostgreSQL pools; and exits. Memory-pressure shutdown reuses this coordinator while retaining its explicit non-zero cause.

**Threat and controls.** The credible threats are denial of service from restart loops or false watchdog kills, spoofed or stale lifecycle evidence, secret disclosure through logs, and a stale child marking a newer boot clean. Controls are one wrapper mutation boundary, boot-ID-fenced terminal writes, provider-issued correlation IDs, positive-field structured logs that contain no user content or credential values, bounded restart/shutdown budgets, and the existing logger retention/redaction boundary. STRIDE mapping: denial of service, spoofing, and repudiation. Control owners: Engineering and Platform.

**Supervisor health authority.** The wrapper's deep DB/pool probe crosses one bounded parent-to-child trust boundary over loopback. The wrapper generates a new 256-bit capability for each child generation, passes it only through that child's environment, the child consumes and deletes the environment value at module load, and the wrapper authenticates the exact `GET /api/health/supervisor` request with a dedicated header. API policy allows only that exact method and path to reach route-owned loopback and constant-time capability validation; a non-loopback request or missing/invalid capability returns 404 before the database probe runs. The authenticated `/api/health/deep` operator route and the personal `/api/health/*` prefix remain unchanged. Both authorized routes call one response producer and expose only S1 operational metadata: boot ID, uptime, memory totals, bounded database-probe result, and pool counts/saturation reasons. No user, account, session, memory, contact, finance, health, email, or other personal data is read. Healthy polls are excluded from response-body access logs, in-flight wedge accounting, and API performance samples; their successful GET line remains debug-only and suppressed in normal production logging. Degraded and failed probes remain visible through the wrapper's warning/error paths. The credible threats are capability spoofing, health-metadata disclosure, false-kill denial of service, and capability leakage through logs. Deterministic controls are loopback-only transport, per-generation entropy, exact route/method classification, default-deny route validation, constant-time comparison, no capability logging, bounded timeouts, and the unchanged heartbeat-plus-stdout liveness predicate plus independent three-probe pool escalation. STRIDE mapping: spoofing, information disclosure, denial of service, and repudiation. Control owners: Engineering and Platform.

**Boot migration availability boundary.** July 23 stage evidence exposed two deterministic defects behind the supervisor: a project-to-Library data migration consumed the full current `projects` shape before its work-vault schema producer ran, and Library bootstrap recreated retired `type`/`metadata` columns that route registration removed. PostgreSQL retains dropped attributes and counts them toward the 1,600-column table limit, so that add/drop cycle exhausted `library_pages`. The migration rejection escaped the startup promise, leaving the child alive until the wrapper reported a misleading 90-second timeout. Controls now order vault/work schema producers before consumers; serialize the one-time migration across replicas; honor its durable completion record; use a bounded explicit projection; preserve project owner/account/vault on migrated pages; give retired columns one terminal cleanup owner; and make the startup promise log content-free fatal evidence and exit nonzero immediately. Assets: A02/A03/A06/A08. Threats: cross-vault attachment, replayed partial migration, schema-drift denial of service, and repudiation through misleading watchdog evidence. Control owners: Identity and Data, Application and Platform, Operations. Security result: closed in source. Residual risk: the stage relation retains historical dropped-column slots until a deliberate table rewrite or logical restore; stopping the contradictory DDL prevents further growth. Rollback is the merged PR revert.

**Residual provider boundary.** A process with same-container environment or memory access can recover the capability, but already has equivalent or greater runtime authority; the capability is not a defense against a compromised container. In-process code cannot observe or prove whole-container SIGKILL, kernel/provider OOM eviction, host migration, native termination that kills the wrapper, or another provider stop after the fact. A next boot records an active predecessor without matching supervisor evidence only as `unclean` with unknown cause. Railway documentation states that deployment replacement sends SIGTERM then SIGKILL after `RAILWAY_DEPLOYMENT_DRAINING_SECONDS`, whose default is zero, and that deployment health checks do not continuously monitor a live service. Provider draining time, restart policy, metrics, and instance events remain Platform-owned evidence. Rollback is under five minutes by reverting the merged PR or restoring the prior wrapper health path; no destructive schema migration is required.

## 11.16 Workflow terminal-state integrity review, July 22, 2026

Workflow stage children, user-owned build artifacts, and autonomous execution capacity cross F05 and the child-to-parent orchestration boundary. Runtime evidence from the Meetings Page workflow exposed four availability and integrity gaps: the parent monitor converted every saved uncheckpointed child into `passed` even when the child declared failure; child-created Library pages stayed only in `session_artifacts`, leaving downstream stages without the approved specification; an undefined failure transition terminalized the attempt before throwing and left the run active without work; and generic sparse-argument normalization removed the explicit empty replacement required for an exact deletion edit.

**Closed in source.** Terminal child reconciliation now requires one unambiguous explicit `Outcome:` discriminant and fails closed otherwise. The atomic stage-completion winner projects only principal-visible artifacts linked to the exact child session into attempt-bound workflow artifacts before transition, with a per-attempt transaction lock and deduplication on run, attempt, reference type, and reference ID. Built-in Design and Implement failure transitions are explicit; any future incomplete template settles the run as blocked with a structured failure packet. Empty optional values remain absent by default, while the two exact-edit actions preserve an explicit empty `new_string` as their bounded deletion contract. Library mutation success awaits its session-artifact link so workflow projection cannot race the producer.

Assets: A02/A08 user-owned workflow state, artifacts, and execution capacity. Threats: LLM06/LLM10 approval bypass, ambiguous stage control, zombie or repeated autonomous work, cross-user artifact adoption, a stage child mutating another run or attempt, and generated objectives widening the user-authorized work. Controls: one parsed result discriminant, principal-scoped session-artifact reads, attempt-bound projection, atomic completion claim, total transition handling, and deterministic workflow-stage authority. `workflow_sessions.role = stage_attempt` now binds each child to one exact run and attempt before first context assembly; the workflows handler derives IDs from that relation and exposes only run inspection, evidence attachment/capture, and terminal completion. The originating user message is captured once as a scoped workflow artifact and remains authoritative over generated objective prose. No route, permission, principal, provider, schema, or data class changes. Security result: no new open finding. Rollback is the merged PR revert; no schema or user-data migration is required. Residual risk: proportionality remains model judgment, while unauthorized workflow mutation is deterministic and fail-closed.

## 11.17 Dynamic application heap budget, July 22, 2026

**SEC-2026-034, high, closed in source.** Production runtime evidence confirmed an A08 availability failure at B11/B12: concurrent large-context Agent runs drove the application child into V8 heap exhaustion during `JSON.stringify`, briefly returning 502 responses and interrupting active work. The supervisor had hard-coded `--max-old-space-size=2048`, so a larger Railway instance could not use its available memory. Allocating the entire container to V8 would create a second failure mode because Buffers, native modules, worker stacks, code space, child-process overhead, and the OS consume memory outside old space.

The canonical control remains `server/process-wrapper.ts`. Before each application-child start, the wrapper reads the Linux cgroup v2 or v1 memory limit, bounds it by host memory, assigns 75% to V8 old space, and passes the resolved MiB value directly on the `dist/index.mjs` Node command line. The direct argument overrides ambient `NODE_OPTIONS` without granting the same large heap to descendant Node processes. The remaining 25% is explicit native and co-resident headroom. The existing Railway service-instance RSS watchdog still exits gracefully at 90% of the provider limit, preserving restart evidence and active-work recovery. `/api/health/deep` reports V8's actual `heap_size_limit` rather than parsing or assuming a flag value, and `child_started` logs the resolved heap, available memory, percentage, and cgroup source.

Controls: AGENT-04, EXEC-03, OBS-02, REC-02; OWASP API4 and LLM10 unbounded consumption. Owner: Agent Runtime Owner with Application and Platform Owner. Closure evidence: production OOM logs from deployment `568e4260-7c9d-46f1-9ec0-de1f1d522faa`, `server/process-wrapper.ts`, `server/routes/setup.ts`, impact review, production build, and merged PR. Residual risk: raising the ceiling does not cure an unbounded producer; oversized serialization and concurrency remain separately observable and should be reduced at their canonical producers. Rollback is the merged PR revert; no schema or live data mutation is involved.

**Post-deployment evidence preservation.** Two optional inference-payload capture writes failed at a nested `SAVEPOINT sp1`, and later direct inserts exposed PostgreSQL-incompatible `U+0000` data in otherwise valid provider requests. The capture boundary records a bounded allowlist from up to five nested causes: error name/message, SQLSTATE code, severity, detail, hint, position, internal position/query, server where, schema/table/column/data type/constraint, server file/line, and routine. It also records provider, boundary, model, activity, session, attempt, and whether an ambient database transaction existed when capture was attempted. Stacks, query parameters, captured requests, credentials, and arbitrary error fields remain excluded. Capture remains fail-open and recovered failure is warning-level. Durable capture now exits the ambient transaction context before opening its own general-lane transaction, while lossless request encoding removes the known JSONB incompatibility. This preserves OBS-02 evidence without changing provider dispatch authority.

## 11.18 Per-object work authorization, July 22, 2026

**SEC-WORK-001, critical before external work sharing, closed in source.** The affected assets are S1-S3 project, milestone, and task rows plus sharing authority and its audit history. The boundary is an authenticated User principal crossing account ownership to one explicitly shared object. Credible threats include forged subject/object IDs, stale caches, route-local guard gaps, capability-ordering errors, vault co-membership disclosure, parent traversal, destructive action by a writer, and repudiation. STRIDE coverage: spoofing, tampering, information disclosure, elevation of privilege, and repudiation. OWASP coverage: API1, API3, API5; IAM-02, IAM-03, DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-03.

`object_grants` is the single typed recipient ledger. Every canonical project, milestone, and task read or mutation composes its owner visibility authority with one correlated live-grant `EXISTS`; `write` includes read and only `admin` adds re-sharing, definition edits, deletion, and reassignment. Vault identity never grants recipient access. `object-grant-service.ts` serializes grant mutation, verifies target admin authority, retains revoked rows with `revoked_at`, and writes the privileged audit record inside the same transaction. Work deletion revokes live grants transactionally. Direct attachment and dashboard consumers use the same predicate. Process-local work authorization caches were removed so revocation is database-authoritative on the next read. Migration and boot convergence create constrained live indexes for bounded lookup.

P3 extends this boundary to the pre-account state. `invited_subjects` is a global data-minimized principal keyed by one structurally normalized email and contains no credentials, profile, account, Vault, or Person data. Task and grant mutation accepts an invited email only long enough to resolve or create the opaque subject ID under a global advisory lock. Registration normalizes the submitted email through the same auth helper; only a valid single-use invitation token with an exact normalized email match is the verified claim event. Public registration may create a User when explicitly enabled, but it cannot claim invited access until a separate verified-email boundary exists. The User write, every live grant rebind, every invited task-assignment rebind, the claimed subject stamp, and the privileged audit record commit in one transaction. Revoked grants remain revoked and are never rebound. Existing live User-grant collisions resolve to the stronger capability while preserving one-live-grant uniqueness. Replays preserve the original `claimed_at`; a claim by a different User fails closed. People and the cross-user People email index are absent from the flow.

Project Vault memberships extend SEC-WORK-001 without weakening direct sharing. `project_vault_memberships` is the owner-visibility authority: an owned Project is readable or writable only when at least one live membership intersects the User principal's persisted visible-Vault set. A direct Project object grant bypasses Vault visibility only for that recipient, while named system authority preserves the deliberate cross-Vault path. Membership replacement requires owner Project admin authority, locks the Project transactionally, accepts at least one distinct visible Vault, preserves existing hidden live memberships, validates the full final set as live and in the same account, replaces the scoped rows atomically, and keeps `projects.vault_id` only as a compatible primary/default projection. New Projects write their first membership in the creation transaction. Project milestones and Project-attached Tasks derive read visibility and their primary container from the parent Project; primary placement changes move child anchors transactionally. Standalone Tasks retain their existing scope/grant semantics. Vault archival fails closed when it would strand a Project without another live membership. Boot converges Project anchors before first Milestone adoption, records durable completion, and never replays rollback-only `projects.milestones` JSON afterward; Project membership backfill runs only after work Vault convergence and fails closed rather than degrading through generic heal. Threats addressed: hidden-Vault enumeration, cross-account membership injection, last-membership removal, partial replacement, stale JSON resurrection, cross-Vault child placement, deterministic boot denial, child-row disclosure after a Project disappears, and accidental removal of explicit recipient access. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, IAM-02, IAM-03, API1, API3, API5, AGENT-03, OBS-02.

Owner: Engineering. Closure evidence: merged source and production build. Active authorization testing remains prohibited by repository policy and requires an explicitly scoped synthetic environment. Rollback is the merged PR revert; additive membership and grant tables may remain unused safely. Residual risk: email ownership can change after an invited-subject claim; grants therefore remain individually revocable before claim, while post-claim access follows the User identity and normal grant revocation path. Project membership acceptance is build/static evidence only until a scoped synthetic multi-user runtime exercise is authorized.

## 11.18 Principal-scoped meeting index, July 22, 2026

Meeting transcripts, participant identities, and recap/agenda pages are A02/S2 user-owned data crossing the authenticated `/api/meetings/records` and model-tool boundaries. The credible threats are cross-user or hidden-Vault disclosure, counting failed join attempts as captured meetings, and a second meeting store drifting from the session-owned identity boundary.

**Closed in source.** The meeting index is a read projection of canonical meeting session documents. `buildConvDocumentMetadata(...)` persists the full `MeetingSessionMeta` and attributed transcript-fragment count on every session write, with an additive migration backfilling historical meeting documents. Metadata enumeration continues through `DocumentStorage.getDocumentsMetadataOnly(...)`, which applies principal/account and visible-Vault scope. Direct fallback hydration uses the same principal-scoped chat store. People resolution uses the scoped People store, Calendar artifacts use sensitive-scope predicates, and direct recap/agenda page hydration independently applies Library visibility. Only `botStatus=ended` sessions enter completed counts; a meeting has notes only when persisted attributed transcript fragments exist, so lobby timeouts and failed joins are excluded. Routes remain behind the existing `/api/meetings` authentication prefix, and the meetings tool calls the same projection under the current principal. No public route, permission, provider, external data flow, or second meeting table is introduced. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-03, OBS-02. Security result: no open finding. Residual risk is inherited from the canonical session owner/Vault predicates and meeting transcript attribution. Rollback is the merged PR revert; the metadata backfill is additive and safe to leave in place.

## 11.16c vNext predictive relationship graph, July 23, 2026

The affected assets are A02 private memory, A03 durable agent state, and A07 provenance evidence across F03/F06/F12 and B06/B09/B16. Typed relationship assertion and transition derivation create a poisoning target: a caller could attach another user's evidence, relabel similarity or sequence as causality, replay an edge into greater authority, fan out an unbounded graph, or leak private claim content through diagnostics and logs.

**Closed in source.** `vnext-transition-graph.ts` is the canonical P2 mutation and derivation boundary. Canonical edge writes validate both endpoint claims and every cited source ref under the same writable User principal, require bounded certainty and non-empty bounded provenance, use a stable replay key, and encode semantic, evidence, temporal, causal, and consolidation classes independently. Derived edges require method/version metadata. Database constraints require causal edges to remain `causal_hypothesis`; model-proposed `sourceClaimIndex` produces only an evidence-backed semantic `qualifies` hypothesis. Transition recompute is transaction-locked, bounded, stable-keyed, and deletes only stale rows from its exact method/version. Its observed paths derive only from explicit temporal edges; an optional mechanism is included only from an evidence-traceable causal hypothesis. Every table is owner/account scoped, every read uses visible scope, every write/delete uses writable scope, diagnostics are bounded, and structured logs contain IDs and discriminants rather than claim or source content.

Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-02, AGENT-05, OBS-01, REC-02. Rollback is the merged PR revert; additive columns/tables can remain inert. Residual risk: source-backed causal hypotheses can still be wrong. P3/P4 must keep prediction output shadow-only, measure calibration and reviewed precision, and abstain before any user-facing activation.

## 11.16b vNext orthogonal claim dimensions, July 23, 2026

The affected assets are A02 personal memory, A03 durable agent state, and A07 provenance evidence across F03/F06/F12 and B06/B09/B16. Orthogonal claim diagnostics add source clarity, evidence lineage, relationship certainty, typed-event strength derivation, integration derivation, and explicit temporal applicability. Credible threats are cross-tenant evidence insertion, unbounded certainty poisoning, duplicate source lineage masquerading as independent corroboration, lifecycle stage regaining hidden truth authority, and sensitive evidence content appearing in logs.

**Closed in source.** New evidence fields are additive on existing ownership-scoped relationship rows. `addSourceRef` validates the writable claim before insertion, bounds identifiers/content/provenance, and accepts only 0–1 clarity/certainty. Claim detail reads every relationship and strength event through visible-scope predicates, deduplicates certainty evidence by explicit source lineage, derives Strength only from the typed replay-safe event ledger, and returns a literal null combined score. Extraction confidence remains compatibility data under an explicit name; lifecycle stage is declared non-authoritative. Diagnostics are bounded and structured logs remain content-free.

## 11.16a vNext memory signal integrity repair, July 23, 2026

The affected assets are A02 personal memory, A03 durable agent state, A07 audit evidence, and A08 resource integrity across F03/F06/F12 and B06/B09/B16. Passive context rendering and duplicate extraction previously mutated recall telemetry that also influenced retrieval, confidence decay, and retirement. A cache replay, retry, or quiet period could therefore amplify or suppress a private claim without meaningful evidence.

**Closed in source.** Passive context inclusion now writes an ownership-scoped `memory_vnext_exposures` row keyed by claim plus durable `context_build_id`; retries and cache replay converge on the same row and carry zero strength or certainty. Meaningful reinforcement has one typed `memory_vnext_strength_events` boundary with fixed bounded weights, required replay keys, writable-claim validation, metadata limits, and content-free structured audit fields. Exact and semantic duplicate extraction preserve the existing claim and source references without changing recall, strength, or certainty. Legacy recall columns remain readable compatibility telemetry but are frozen and excluded from retrieval, lifecycle, retirement, and deletion. Re-extraction absence and retrieval silence no longer decay confidence or retire claims.

Controls: DATA-01, AGENT-02, AGENT-05, OBS-01, REC-02. Threats addressed: cross-tenant event insertion, replay amplification, arbitrary-weight memory poisoning, passive-use feedback loops, and sensitive-content leakage in logs. Rollback is the merged PR revert; the additive event tables may remain inert. Residual risk: P0 records typed strength events but does not yet activate a strength projection in retrieval. That controlled activation belongs to the next orthogonal-dimensions phase.

## 11.19 Speech-recognition environment binding and attempt identity, July 29, 2026

**Status:** Foundation and unified Scribe/Deepgram meeting transport controls complete; Speechmatics activation remains intentionally gated.  
**Severity:** High before controls; Low residual while source policy continues constraining Recall/native routing to Scribe/Deepgram.  
**Owner:** Speech Recognition / Platform Environment.  
**SLA:** Before any Speechmatics activation or database binding is allowed to route meeting audio.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A02/S1 raw meeting audio and S2 transcript, A03 meeting state, A05 provider credentials, A07 operational evidence, A08 realtime availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B08, B14, B16.

**Threat:** An arbitrary or cross-environment capability binding could select the wrong provider account, alter raw-audio egress, expose a credential through client/session/log state, merge acoustic speakers across reconnects, or turn shared-microphone chronology into Person identity.

**Deterministic controls:** `server/speech-recognition/` owns a strict versioned, discriminated allowlist for Scribe, Deepgram, and Speechmatics configs. The typed system-permission-gated Platform Environment route is the only speech mutation boundary; the generic capability API excludes speech writes and reads. A binding-aware resolver joins the exact environment, binding, and active matching Provider Connection before decryption, rejects binding-owned secrets, and accepts only global/system platform-managed credentials. Runtime adapters receive credentials explicitly, current messages retain secret-free immutable attempt/binding/model/config provenance, and machine speaker keys are scoped to random provider-session attempt IDs. Native shared microphones remain audio sources only: creators stay roster participants but are not bound to the microphone or first cluster. One `SpeechRecognitionStreamCoordinator` now owns Recall and native candidate startup, protocol readiness, a 512 KiB/5-second audio budget, visible overflow failure, serialized final delivery, same-binding reconnect with a fresh attempt ID, no post-commit provider switching, and bounded provider/consumer drain. Enabled environment bindings supersede the rolling legacy secret for their exact adapter kind; the legacy candidate is projected only when no enabled binding exists, so configuration cannot hide an unreported second credential path. Source policy still selects only Scribe for participant streams and Deepgram for shared rooms during migration. Scribe emits an explicit commit before closing; Deepgram emits `CloseStream` and waits for terminal metadata/server close. Safe logs exclude audio, transcript, credentials, provider error bodies, and close reasons.

**Rollout evidence/prerequisites:** Migration `0106_speech_recognition_foundation.sql` and boot convergence add only `sort_order` plus an index; old meeting documents remain readable because all provenance fields are optional. Stage and Live routing remain unchanged until the coordinator rollout begins reading enabled environment bindings. Before activation, create platform-managed Provider Connections, configure Stage bindings through the typed API, keep Speechmatics disabled, verify protocol/config without content-bearing logs, and preserve Live priority until same-audio quality evidence clears the promotion gate.

**Residual risk:** Speechmatics has config/control-plane representation but no implemented registry adapter yet, so selection fails closed. Source-policy adapter constraints deliberately prevent an enabled Speechmatics binding from changing current Scribe/Deepgram meeting routing in this step; the next implementation step must add that adapter before controlled Stage activation. Production build proves compilation, not real provider timing or tail-drain behavior, so authenticated Stage acceptance remains required. Functional and quality promotion evidence remain pending by design.

## 11.20 Consented retained meeting-audio and Stage replay, July 29, 2026

**Status:** Privacy-safe foundation complete; no sample exists without explicit versioned owner consent, and provider promotion remains evidence-gated.  
**Severity:** Critical before controls; Low residual while retention is native-capture opt-in and replay is Stage/system-permission gated.  
**Owner:** Meetings / Speech Recognition / Object Storage.  
**SLA:** Controls are release-blocking before any raw meeting-audio retention or replay is enabled.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A02/S1 raw meeting audio and S2 transcript/evaluation output, A03 meeting and consent state, A05 provider credentials, A07 audit evidence, A08 realtime/storage availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B08, B14, B16.

**Threat:** A caller could retain audio without informed consent, hide recording state after navigation, export or delete another user/Vault's sample, keep raw audio indefinitely, exhaust memory/storage, replay into Live or an arbitrary provider credential, overwrite the canonical transcript with evaluation output, leak plaintext audio/transcripts through object storage or logs, or race deletion against an active evaluation.

**Deterministic controls:** The native creation route defaults retention off and accepts it only with `retentionConsent=true`, consent version 1, and a 1–7 day horizon before the audio WebSocket opens. The consent dialog names raw audio, encryption, evaluation purpose, export/delete, and the seven-day bound; canonical Meeting metadata visibly projects recording/ready/failure state across reconnects. `meeting_audio_samples` and `meeting_audio_evaluations` structurally require user scope, owner, account, Vault, fixed PCM format, bounded size/duration, explicit lifecycle status, and replay-safe uniqueness. `meeting/audio-retention.ts` is the sole mutation boundary: it retains only PCM accepted by live recognition, caps capture at 32 MiB/15 minutes, application-encrypts bytes with AES-256-GCM before the canonical Vault-aware private object upload, verifies object persistence, and records original immutable recognition attempts. Owner export scopes the row before object access, decrypts server-side, verifies byte count, returns private/no-store WAV, and never mints a storage URL. Owner delete and canonical Meeting delete remove evaluation objects, sample objects, ACLs, and then tombstone rows; active evaluation blocks deletion. Hourly expiry discovers 25 metadata rows under the named `meeting-audio-expiry` principal, then restores each exact owner/account/Vault before deletion. Replay requires `system:write`, one explicit visible environment named Stage, one exact enabled binding resolved through the canonical binding-aware credential boundary, fixed shared-room format, bounded pacing/write deadlines and utterance count, and a replay-safe idempotency key. Replay output is a separate application-encrypted artifact carrying original and replay provenance; it never calls `ingestMeetingEvent(...)` or mutates meeting messages. Logs contain only IDs, counts, timings, status/failure discriminants, and safe error types—never audio, transcript text, provider bodies, speaker labels, credentials, object URLs, or raw keys in client state.

**Rollback and residual risk:** Revert the merged PR; additive tables can remain inert and encrypted objects remain owner-deletable/expiry-bound. The retained-sample advisory transaction lock serializes replay claims against deletion, replay idempotency converges through its owner/account uniqueness constraint, and deletion tombstones rows before object I/O so partial storage failure remains retryable without making bytes eligible for a new replay. Canonical object-upload logs retain only byte count, category, and Vault-scope state rather than private raw object keys. Application encryption currently buffers a bounded sample in memory for finalization/export/replay, so the 32 MiB ceiling is a hard operational control rather than streaming encryption. The first release retains only explicitly started native shared-room capture; Recall retention requires a separate consent UX and transport review. A completed evaluation proves functionality, not comparative quality. Speechmatics/other provider promotion still requires manually labeled same-audio evidence for speaker confusion, switch errors, cluster count, transcript completeness, latency, and unknown-speaker rate.

## 11.18 Plan and Workflow child retry fencing, July 25, 2026

A03 durable orchestration state, A04 autonomous mutation authority, and A08 availability/cost cross F05 and B11 when a parent monitor times out, loses, or observes a prematurely failed child session. The confirmed defect allowed the parent to request abort, project the child as failed, and start a successor while `AgentExecutor` could still own the prior mutating run. This enabled duplicate People/profile mutations, contradictory audit entries, replay amplification, and false terminal state.

**Closed in source.** `child-session-monitor.ts` is the single abort-and-confirm boundary: it waits through the executor's bounded drain budget until no matching active run remains, serializes monitor settlement, and returns `termination_unconfirmed` when liveness cannot be disproven. Plans pause and Workflows block without retry on that outcome. Plan automatic retry, manual resume, and abandoned-child closure reuse the same fence. Principal, Plan lease, attempt ownership, and tool authorization are unchanged.

Controls: AGENT-04, OBS-01, DATA-01, REC-02. Owner: Agent Runtime / Orchestration. Severity: high. Rollback is the merged PR revert. Residual risk: an uncooperative child can force a fail-closed pause/block requiring later recovery, but cannot authorize an overlapping retry.

That combination risked missing required per-vault defaults and attempting cross-vault mutation.

Cleanup still requires exact untouched legacy signatures, preserving user-created or modified rows. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, AGENT-03. Owner: Business / Metrics. Severity: medium. Residual risk: modified legacy seeded rows remain intentionally because provenance is insufficient to distinguish them safely from user-owned data.

## 11.17 Principal-scoped DOCX attachment reads, July 22, 2026

Uploaded DOCX files are A02/S2 data crossing F07/B07 and B10 from private object storage into a model-invoked read tool. The prior DOCX reader accepted only workspace paths, so canonical attachment paths failed even when upload and ACL state were valid. A naive repair using public URLs or direct raw storage keys would create an IDOR and disclosure risk.

**Closed in source.** `docx.read` now discriminates exact `/objects/...` entity paths from workspace paths. Object-backed reads require the current authenticated user principal, resolve through `ObjectStorageService`, enforce the existing object ACL with `ObjectPermission.READ`, cap the compressed input at 25 MB and declared uncompressed archive content at 100 MB, and parse the authorized buffer directly. Missing principal, foreign object, absent ACL, missing object, and oversized document all fail closed. Controls: DATA-01, DATA-04, ING-01, AGENT-03. Security result: no open finding. Rollback is the merged PR revert; no schema or data mutation is involved.

### A74: App error telemetry discarded exception identity and over-collected navigation data

- **Status:** Resolved in code on 2026-08-06; pending production deployment verification.
- **Severity:** Medium observability and data-minimization defect. Global browser error and unhandled-rejection producers passed only structured objects to the shared logger, so canonical aggregation could not derive a stable error code or producer callsite. Their runtime context also included query strings, hashes, and full URLs that can contain S2 private identifiers or tokens.
- **Assets / boundaries:** A02/S2 client navigation state and runtime failures cross F02/F06 into authenticated client-log ingestion and B03 operational telemetry.
- **Threats:** Exception payloads or navigation parameters disclose private data in logs (STRIDE information disclosure / DATA-01), while flattened errors prevent reliable incident diagnosis and recovery (OBS-01).
- **Controls / owner:** The App boundary now normalizes every thrown or rejected value into a real `Error`, assigns deterministic `APP_WINDOW_ERROR` or `APP_UNHANDLED_REJECTION` codes when the producer supplied none, preserves the original cause, and records bounded operation, route pathname, source coordinates, and crash correlation. Query, hash, and full URL fields are removed. Owner: Client Platform / Reliability.
- **SLA:** Immediate. Closed in source with the production build required before merge.
- **Residual risk:** Third-party exception messages and stacks may still contain provider-authored sensitive text already governed by client-log redaction policy; source maps determine final bundle-to-source precision.

### A68: Vault administration page mounted a system-admin migration control for ordinary users

- **Status:** Resolved in code on 2026-08-05; pending production deployment verification.
- **Severity:** Medium availability and least-privilege defect. The server correctly denied the request, but the client exposed an inoperable privileged control and surfaced the resulting `system:write` denial on the ordinary Vault workflow.
- **Assets / data classes / boundaries:** Vault metadata and legacy migration state (S2); authenticated account principal boundary; browser-to-API boundary; system-administrator permission boundary.
- **Credible abuse / threat:** A non-admin authenticated user opening Vaults could invoke `GET /api/vaults/migration`. The deterministic server guard prevented privilege escalation, but the client generated unauthorized traffic and could mislead users into treating a privileged migration failure as a Vault creation failure (STRIDE: elevation attempt denied; availability/usability degradation; agentic risk from ambiguous authorization errors).
- **Canonical control and owner:** `server/routes/vault-routes.ts` remains the authoritative `system:write` enforcement boundary for migration operations. `client/src/pages/vaults-admin.tsx` now composes `VaultMigrationControls` only when `useAuth().hasPermission("system:write")` is true. Client gating is defense in depth and UX integrity, never authorization. Owner: Sharing/Vaults.
- **Evidence:** Production screenshot from 2026-08-05 showed `403 ... permission: system:write` on the Vaults page for an ordinary user; route inspection identified `VaultMigrationControls` as the only Vault-page caller of a `system:write`-guarded endpoint; impact analysis found a single low-risk contribution surface.
- **Residual risk / SLA:** Verify the repaired Vaults page with a non-admin principal after deployment and confirm that creating a Vault reaches `POST /api/vaults` without any migration request. Until deployment, the server continues to fail closed. Target: 2026-08-06.

### A69: Model provider failures lost deterministic source identity at the telemetry boundary

**Status:** Mitigated in code (2026-08-06); production build required before release. **Severity:** Medium. **Owner:** Agent Runtime / Security Program Owner. **SLA:** Before the next production release. **Assets/data:** A02/S0-S3 model prompts and responses, A03 session/run state, A07 telemetry, A08 provider availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05. **Threat:** Provider, transport, or local adapter failures can contain untrusted payload text or non-`Error` thrown values. Logging only an interpolated message discards the producer stack and stable code, collapsing distinct failures into `ModelClient:Error:UNCLASSIFIED`; logging full provider payloads would instead risk exposing prompt, response, credential, or endpoint data. This is an observability-integrity and information-disclosure risk (STRIDE repudiation/information disclosure; LLM supply-chain payload risk). **Deterministic controls:** The canonical `chatCompletion` catch normalizes every escaped value through `enrichModelError`, preserves the existing bounded provider/client classification, and passes that structured `Error` to the shared logger so `recordApplicationError` derives a stable code and safe source callsite. Boundary inference-tracking failures normalize non-`Error` values, assign `INFERENCE_TRACKING_FAILED`, and provide a producer stack. Human-readable logs remain limited to existing provider/model/routing identifiers and normalized messages; provider payloads, prompts, responses, headers, credentials, and URLs are not added. Expected aborts remain debug-level and do not enter error telemetry. **Residual risk:** Upstream SDK messages may still include provider-authored text already permitted by existing logging policy; source maps can improve source precision but are not required for deterministic classification. Provider-specific adapters remain responsible for redacting secrets before constructing errors.

### A67: Oversized tool output could bypass model-context and logging bounds

**Status:** Mitigated in code (2026-08-05); production build verified. **Severity:** High. **Owner:** Agent Runtime / Security Program Owner. **SLA:** Release-blocking for model-context injection paths. **Assets/data:** A02/S0-S3 retrieved and external tool content, A03 session/run state, A07 telemetry, A08 model-context availability. **Flows:** F02, F03, F06, F12. **Boundaries:** B03, B04, B05, B06, B14. **Threat:** A tool or external provider can return unexpectedly large, adversarial, secret-bearing, or malformed content; pagination is not a security boundary. Unbounded insertion can exhaust context, amplify prompt injection, or leak payloads through logs. Archive failure could otherwise cause raw fallback reinjection. **Deterministic controls:** Every model-facing tool result crosses the shared `maybeOffloadToolOutput` admission boundary. Inline admission is independently capped by both characters and estimated tokens; oversized/binary output is durably scoped through `ContentIndexer`, replaced with a compact canonical `@file` manifest, section handles, untrusted-content framing, and bounded retrieval guidance. Errors do not bypass admission. Archive failure fails closed with structured recovery metadata and no raw payload. Telemetry records only tool/action identifiers, disposition, size/token counts, run/session IDs, and artifact identity; it never records result content or secrets. `indexed_content` remains responsible for authenticated, user-scoped artifact retrieval. **Configuration:** `TOOL_OUTPUT_INLINE_CHAR_BUDGET=12000`, `TOOL_OUTPUT_INLINE_TOKEN_BUDGET=3000`, `TOOL_OUTPUT_PREVIEW_CHAR_BUDGET=1200`; environment overrides may only tune within the same central boundary. **Residual risk:** Token estimation is approximate; a future executor that bypasses the shared projector could evade admission; retrieved sections remain untrusted data and require the existing model instruction hierarchy and artifact authorization controls.

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** High before controls; Low residual.
**Owner:** Goal System / Identity and Data.
**SLA:** Closed before Goal Vault ownership shipped.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A03 Goal state, A04 Vault/account relationships. **Flows:** authenticated Goal create, read, list, and update. **Boundaries:** B01 application authorization and B04 persistence.

**Threat:** A caller could omit Vault ownership or submit an inaccessible Vault ID, leaving Goals outside the Vault model or attaching Goal metadata to another account's context. Client-only selection would not protect tool, API, migration, or background creation paths.

Every Goal resolves exactly one `vaultId`, defaults missing ownership to the principal's active authorized Vault (then first visible Vault), rejects inaccessible Vault IDs, validates persisted metadata during reads, and projects the field to every list consumer. The shared create/update schemas expose only one scalar Vault ID, so multi-membership cannot be represented.

**Evidence:** `shared/models/goals.ts`, `server/goal-storage.ts`, `client/src/components/goal-inline-details.tsx`, and `client/src/pages/goals.tsx`. Control mapping: AUTHZ-01, DATA-03, INPUT-01. **Residual risk:** Legacy Goals are assigned when next read rather than through an eager bulk migration; this is deterministic per principal and writes back immediately.

## 11.21 Git clone source-binding authority, August 6, 2026

Git clone crosses B03/B08 and F11 over A08/S0-S2: it invokes a credentialed external executable, retrieves provider-controlled source, and creates a session-owned mutable workspace. The prior contract accepted an arbitrary repository URL plus optional environment/connection hints, selected credentials from those coordinates, and could fall back to legacy Git credentials. A model, stale producer, or retrieved prompt could therefore request an unintended repository, create repository/environment mismatch, or cause credentials to be selected outside the canonical Platform source-binding identity.

**Closed in source.** Normal `git.clone` accepts no caller-owned repository coordinates and resolves exactly one active GitHub binding named `Mantra / Web / stage`. A positive `platformEnvironmentId` on `clone` or `clone_from_environment` selects that Platform Environment through the same resolver (the transitional `clone + platformEnvironmentId` shape is absorbed rather than rejected). Both paths derive URL, provider connection, credential, repository name, and branch exclusively from `environment_source_bindings`; stale caller-owned `url`, `directory`, or `branch` values are rejected, and dumped empty routing fields / non-positive IDs are treated as absence. Clone has no legacy credential fallback, uses `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, writes only to the session-owned `repos/` namespace, logs bounded source identity without credentials, removes partial destinations on failure, and reuses the immutable shared dependency image after clone. Controls: AUTHZ-01, EXEC-01, EXEC-02, DATA-03, AUDIT-01. Owner: Agent Runtime / Git Tools. Severity: Medium before fix; closed in source. **Residual risk:** Platform administrators can still misbind an environment or provider connection; provider compromise remains upstream; environment selection remains limited to the canonical Mantra default and an explicit Platform Environment ID.

## 11.23 Legacy chat Vault recovery and DB error classification, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Identity and Data / Database Runtime.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A01 identity, A03 Session/document ownership, A04 Vault/account relationships, A07 telemetry. **Flows:** boot schema convergence, Session Vault ownership repair, application-error aggregation. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability.

**Threat:** Boot recovery for legacy user chat documents joined `users.account_id`, a column that does not exist. PostgreSQL SQLSTATE `42703` aborted the recovery path, left nullable Vault ownership unrepaired, and emitted DB query-contract failures whose logger path discarded the original Error object. Aggregate identity then collapsed to message-token codes (`QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED_AFTER`) without a stable producer code or SQLSTATE, delaying diagnosis (STRIDE: availability/repudiation). Logging full SQL parameters or provider messages would risk information disclosure.

**Deterministic controls:** `ensureVaults` recovers chat ownership only through the canonical `memberships` → personal `accounts` → default `vaults` join and never invents a `users.account_id` column. `server/db.ts` continues to throw the original Postgres error unchanged for callers, while query-contract and unexpected pool-connection error logs attach a telemetry-only `Error` with stable codes `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED` / `UNEXPECTED_POOL_CONNECTION_ERROR`, phase, lane/subsystem/label, pool counts, fingerprint, and SQLSTATE. EnsureVaults terminal failures classify as `FAILED_TO_ENSURE_VAULT_SCHEMA`. No SQL parameters, credentials, principal identity, or arbitrary error payloads are added. Controls: DATA-03, OBS-01, AUDIT-01.

**Evidence:** Live env #12 log `code=42703` on the chat Vault recovery UPDATE; `shared/schema.ts` users/memberships/accounts; `server/migrations/ensure-vaults.ts`; `server/db.ts` instrumentation. **Residual risk:** Pre-existing generic DB instrumentation may still emit bounded SQL text snippets (documented known inconsistency); this repair does not widen that surface. Historical UNCLASSIFIED aggregates cannot be retroactively enriched.

## 11.24 Pool-acquire timeout classification and bounded retry, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Identity and Data / Database Runtime.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A07 operational telemetry, A08 database availability. **Flows:** ordinary `pool.query` on general/voice lanes under load or brief network stalls. **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability.

**Threat:** node-postgres pool acquisition failures (`timeout exceeded when trying to connect`, connect `ETIMEDOUT`) escaped as generic internal errors. Telemetry either kept Node codes or collapsed into `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED`, so operators could not distinguish pool pressure/connect stalls from SQL contract failures, and every acquire miss failed hard with no safe retry (STRIDE: availability/repudiation; OBS-01/DATA-02). Logging SQL parameters, credentials, or principal identity would add disclosure risk.

**Deterministic controls:** `isPoolAcquireTimeoutError` in `server/postgres-errors.ts` is the single detector. Instrumented general/voice `pool.query` classifies terminal acquire failures as `POOL_ACQUIRE_TIMEOUT` (not `QUERY_CONTRACT_FAILED` or raw `ETIMEDOUT`), retains lane/subsystem/label/pool counts/attempt/phase/SQLSTATE only, and retries acquire timeout **once** immediately because no application SQL has started. Statement timeouts, lock timeouts, constraint errors, and mid-query disconnects remain single-shot. Callers still receive the original thrown error. No SQL text parameters, secrets, or principal fields are added. Controls: OBS-01, AUDIT-01, DATA-02.

**Evidence:** Issue 1786061326715 (five concurrent connect-timeout exceptions); live DB op-summary pool samples; `server/db.ts` instrumentation; `server/postgres-errors.ts`. **Residual risk:** Auth-pool and dedicated-client paths remain outside this instrumentation; sustained pool exhaustion still fails after one retry and requires capacity/query diagnosis rather than larger blind retries.

## 11.25 Intentional silent session completion vs empty_response, August 6, 2026

**Status:** Closed in source.
**Severity:** Medium before fix; Low residual.
**Owner:** Agent Runtime / Communications.
**SLA:** Immediate.

**Assets/data:** A02/S2 private Session and tool evidence, A03 durable assistant/Session state, A07 terminal audit evidence. **Flows:** autonomous Skill runs that intentionally end via `session.end` / `set_status=saved` without final assistant narration (e.g. enrich-email). **Boundaries:** B03 persistence, B06 observability, B08 model/tool loop.

**Threat:** After successful tool work and intentional terminal session status, the executor continued the model loop. Provider empty `end_turn` responses were then classified `empty_response` degradation even though the skill had already completed its contract. That produced false-red EmailEnrichment skill runs, wasted empty-final-turn retries, and obscured whether enrichment tool work succeeded (STRIDE: tampering/repudiation/availability analogue; DATA-01/AGENT-04/OBS-01). Expanding diagnostics with transcript or email bodies would add disclosure risk.

**Deterministic controls:** Successful `session.end` and `set_status=saved` emit controlled continuation `session_complete`. `AgentExecutor` stops the loop on that discriminant (and defensively when a pending saved session end already exists), sets `intentionallyCompletedSession`, and excludes that path from `empty_response` / empty-final-turn retries. True empty completions without intentional terminal status remain degraded. Terminal metadata stays bounded (reason/counts/IDs only); no transcript, tool payload, or email content is added. Principal/Vault scope, tool authority, and provider routing are unchanged. Controls: OBS-01, AUDIT-01, AGENT-04.

**Evidence:** Issue 1786061327389; live logs showing enrich-email-class runs with `degradationReason=empty_response`, `emptyFinalTurnRetries=2`, nonzero toolCalls, `contentLen=0`; `server/agent-executor.ts`; `server/bridge-tools.ts` session end/status handlers. **Residual risk:** Skills that neither call `session.end`/`set_status=saved` nor produce final text still degrade as `empty_response` by design; provider empty turns after incomplete work remain visible.
